7～9 月期実質GDP は年率＋1.0％と 
プラス成長を予想（1 次QE）

○ 2026年7～9月期の実質GDP（1次速報値）は前期比＋0.2％（年率＋1.0％）と、4～6月期（前期比＋
0.4％）からやや減速するものの、4四半期連続でのプラス成長を予想 
○ 個人消費が伸び悩む一方、設備投資や民間在庫が押し上げる見込み。なお、公的在庫と輸入では
中東情勢を主因とする一時的な変動が生じ、成長率にプラス・マイナス両面で影響 
○ 年度後半の日本経済は、小幅なプラス成長を予想。インフレ再加速を受け消費抑制が続く一方、設
備投資が堅調に拡大。日本銀行は12月会合で政策金利を1.50％に引き上げる見込み

7～9月期の実質GDP（1次
速報値）はプラス成長 を
予想 
11月16日 （月） に、 内閣府から2026年7～9月期の 「四半期別GDP速 報（QE）」
（1次速報値）が公表される。現時点で入手可能な基礎統計の結果を踏まえ
ると、7～9月期の実質GDPは前期比＋0.2％（ 年 率＋1.0％） となる見込みだ。
4～6月期の前期比＋0.4％（年率＋1.4％）からやや減速するものの、4四半
期連続でプラス成長になると予想する（図表）。 
7～9月期は、内需の柱である個人消費が伸び悩む一方、 設備投資の拡大や、
製造業を中心とする在庫積み増しが成長率を押し上げるとみられる。その他
では、前期の備蓄原油放出の反動が公的在庫投資のプラス要因として現れる
が、原油の代替調達進展を反映した輸入の増加がマイナス要因になるなど、 
図表 2026 年7～9 月期GDP（1 次速報）予測

前期比に対する寄与度（％Pt）

### Page 2

2

中東情勢による一時的な変動も引き続き成長率に影響する見込みだ。以下、
需要項目別に7～9月期の経済動向を詳しく確認しよう。 
個人消費は伸び悩み 。耐
久財の特需剥落や、 根強
い節約志向が影響 
個人消費は前期比▲0.1％（4～6月期：同＋0.0％） と、2四半期連続で伸び
悩む結果になろう。消費の弱さの背景には、 複合的な要因があると考えられ
る。 
耐久財では、自動車とエアコンの「特需」剥落が押し下げ要因になろう。
自動車は、購入時にかかる自動車税の環境性能割が3月末に廃止されたこと
を受け、4月から自動車販売が過去平均に比べ上振れていたが、8月以降は再
び過去平均並みの水準に戻っている。エアコンは、2027年4月の省エネ基準
引き上げで販売価格が上昇するとの見方から駆け込み需要が生じていたが、
景気ウォッチャー調査のコメントなどをみると、8月に入った後は一服した
模様だ。9月は気温が低かったほか、長雨による客足の減少もエアコンなど
季節家電の売り上げに悪影響を及ぼしたとみられる。 
食品やエネルギー、 各種消耗品などの非耐久財では、物価高を背景とする
根強い節約志向により、 弱含みが続いているようだ。今回のインフレ局面が
本格化する前の2020年と比較すると、非耐久消費財の物価は足元までの約6
年半でおよそ1.3倍の水準に上昇した計算になる。こうした累積的な価格上
昇が、 消費者の体感物価の高まりを通じて節約志向を強め、 購入数量の抑制
につながっている可能性がある。 
サービス消費は、 イベント関連支出などで底堅さがみられる一方、日常的
な支出には物価高による節約志向が波及し、 サービスや価格帯、 顧客層によ
る二極化が進みつつあるとみられる。 また、8～9月には台風・長雨による旅
行・ 宿泊のキャンセルや外出控えの影響から、 レジャー関連を中心に消費が
伸び悩んだようだ。 
設備投 資は3四半期ぶり
に前期比プラス 
設備投資は前期比＋1.2％（4～6月 期：同 ▲0.9％） と、3四半期ぶりに前期
比プラスへ転じると予想する。 
直近の設備投資関連指標をみると、資本財の国内向け供給量を表す資本財
総供給指数（除く輸送機械）が7～8月に大きく上昇した。また第3次産業活
動指数では、ソフトウェア業の実質売上高が同じく7～8月に増加したことが
確認でき、どちらも7～9月期の設備投資が拡大したことを示唆している。 
加えて、前期の4～6月期に発生した一時的な押し下げ圧力（特許権の海外
譲渡による国内研究開発投資の減少） がなくなったこともプラス要因だ。た
だし、 これは裏を返せば、7～9月期のQEにおける設備投資の伸びが実態以上
に強い数字になるということでもあり、評価する際には留意が必要である。 
住宅投資は 前期比マイナ
スが継続 
住宅投資は前期比▲0.8％（4～6月 期：同 ▲0.6％ ）と、2四半期連続で前期
比マイナスとなろう。中東情勢を背景とする資材不足は徐々に緩和している
とみられるが、7～8月の住宅着工件数が伸び悩んでおり、住宅価格や金利上

### Page 3

3

昇による需要低迷に加えて、 足元では地震や台風などによる着工 ・ 進捗の遅
れが発生している可能性がある。 
民間 在庫は 2四半期連続
で成長率にプラス寄与 
民間在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度は、＋0.2％Pt（4～6月期：
＋0.3％Pt）とプラスが続く見込みだ。AI関連需要の強さなどを背景に、製
造業で在庫積み増しの動きが進んでいることが影響すると考えられる。な
お、4～6月期は石油化学製品などの供給不足を懸念した前倒し発注が在庫を
押し上げたとみられるが、7～9月期は原油の代替調達進展に伴って供給不足
が緩和したことで、そうした要因の影響は一巡した模様だ。 
公的需要 は備蓄原油放出
の反動で前期比プラスに 
公的需要は前期比＋1.2％（4～6月 期：同 ▲0.8％）と、2四半期ぶりの前期
比プラスを予想する。 
最大の要因は、公的在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度がプラスに
転じるとみられることだ。4～6月期は国家備蓄原油の放出により公的在庫変
動の寄与度が▲0.5％Ptと大幅マイナスになったが、7～9月期は国家原油備
蓄の減少が小幅なものにとどまり（6月末：105日分→9月末：102日分）、公
的在庫の減少ペースが緩和することで、成長率に対し＋0.3％Pt程度のプラ
ス寄与になると見込まれる。 
その他の公的需要項目では、政府消費を前期比＋0.2％（4～6月期：同＋
1.7％）と予想する。4～6月期は高校授業料・小学校給食費の無償化開始に
より高い伸びになったが、7～9月期はそうした特殊要因が剥落し、緩やかな
増加ペースに戻ると考えられる。公共投資は前期比▲0.5％（4～6月期：同
▲0.5％）と、土木関連工事の減少を受けて2四半期連続のマイナスを見込む。 
輸出が小幅に減少する 一
方、 輸入が増加し、外需は
4四半期ぶりのマイナス
寄与 
外需では、輸出が前期比▲0.3％（4～6月期：同＋0.4％）と小幅に減少す
る一方、輸 入が同＋1.3％（4～6月 期：同▲1.7％）と増加に転じる見込みだ。
実質GDP成長率に対する外需の寄与度は▲0.3％Ptと、4四半期ぶりのマイナ
スが予想される。 
輸入の増加は、原油の代替調達進展が主因だ。中東情勢の緊迫化により4
～6月期は一時的に原油輸入が急減したが、7～9月期は米国産原油やホルム
ズ海峡を通過しない中東産原油の調達が増え、9月を除いて前年並みの輸入
量が確保されている。 
輸入はGDPの控除項目であることから、7～9月期は輸入増加が成長率の下
押し要因になる。 ただし、代替調達の進展に伴って国家備蓄原油の放出が抑
制され、 先述したように7～9月期は公的在庫変動が成長率にプラス寄与する
とみられることから、輸入増による成長率押し下げ効果の一部は公的在庫変
動により相殺されるだろう。 
輸出では、前期の4～6月期に特許権の海外譲渡がサービス輸出を押し上げ
た （これは4～6月期の設備投資の押し下げ要因でもある） 反動が現れ、 一時
的に下振れするだろう。一方、旺盛なAI関連需要が支えになり、半導体・電

### Page 4

4

子部品や半導体製造装置などの財輸出は堅調に推移する見込みだ。インバウ
ンド需要 （非居住者家計の国内での直接購入） は、 日中対立の継続による中
国人訪日客の少なさに加え、 航空運賃上昇を受けた欧米訪日客の伸び悩みに
より、低調な結果になると見込まれる。 
2026 年度後半 の実質 GDP
は小幅プラス と予想 。個
人消費が伸び悩む一方、
設備投資が堅調拡大 
以上のとおり、7～9月期の日本経済は前期から減速するものの、4四半期
連続でプラス成長になると予想する。GDPの約半分を占める個人消費の伸び
悩みが足を引っ張っているが、設備投資や製造業を中心とする在庫積み増し
がけん引する格好だ。 
先行きについてみると、2026年度後半（2026年10～2027年3月）の 実質GDP
は、 均してみれば小幅なプラス成長になると予想する。個人消費が振るわな
い一方で、 設備投資の拡大が支えになる構図が続くだろう。2026年度全体の
実質GDP成長率は、前年度末の堅調な伸びで今年度の平均水準が押し上げら
れることもあり（いわゆるプラスのゲタ）、前年比＋1％程度とまずまずの
成長率になる計算だ。 
年度後半の個人消費では、インフレ再加速による実質賃金の伸び悩みが消
費拡大の抑制要因になるだろう。 原油 ・ ナフサ価格高騰の影響が徐々に波及
し、 コア消費者物価指数 （生鮮食品を除く総合） は年度末にかけて前年比＋
3％程度まで上昇すると予想される（ただし、2026年末から政府の電気・ガ
ス料金支援策が実施されれば、0.4～0.5％Pt程度の押し下げ要因になる可能
性がある）。加えて、2027年4月の消費税減税開始前には、保存食品などを
中心とした買い控えの動きが生じるとみられ、 一時的に消費が落ち込む可能
性がある。 
他方で、年度後半の設備投資は堅調に拡大するだろう。日銀短観（9月調
査） では、2026年度の設備投資計画 （ソフトウェア・研究開発を含む設備投
資額 （除く土地投資額）） が前年比＋9.3％と高い伸びを維持した （6月 調 査：
同＋8.8％）。生産・営業用設備投資判断DIが2025年度後半以降に低下し、
設備の不足感が強まっていることをあわせて考えれば、 設備投資は今後拡大
傾向で推移していく見込みだ。 
また、政府の投資支援策である「大胆な投資促進税制」 （特定生産性向上
設備等投資促進税制）が2026年7月末から開始されたことも、今後の設備投
資にとってプラス要因になろう。同税制における減税規模の政府見込みを参
考に試算すると、2026～2028年度に設備投資の前年比変化率を約0.1％Ptず
つ押し上げる効果があると考えられる。 
ただし、 日銀が来年にかけて政策金利を一段と引き上げ、 企業の新規借入
金利がさらに上昇すると予想されること、 また、 投資財の価格が上昇傾向を
続けると考えられることを踏まえると、 中小企業を中心に設備投資を控える
企業が今後増加する可能性がある点には注意が必要だ。

### Page 5

5

日銀は10月会合で 政策金
利を据え置き 。次回利上
げは12月と予想 
10月29・30日に開催された金融政策決定会合において、 日本銀行は政策金
利を現行の1.25％で据え置いた。次の利上げ（1.25％→1.50％） は、次回12
月17・18日の会合と予想する。 前回利上げを行った9月会合に続いて 「3カ月
に1回」ペースでの利上げになり、 日銀の利上げペースが6月以前の 「半年強
に1回」から加速していることを印象付けるものになろう。 
企業の価格転嫁行動は一段と積極化しており、植田総裁が9月会合後の記
者会見で 「政策の局面が変化した」と説明したように、日本銀行は「物価引
き上げ」 から 「物価上振れ抑制」 へと金融政策運営の舵を切っている。12月
の追加利上げ後も、 金利上昇の影響を確認しながら、 日銀は最終的に2027年
半ばにかけて政策金利を2.0％まで引き上げていくだろう。 
なお、経済見通しの詳細については、みずほ総合研究所調査部「2026年・
2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し ─ 転換点は近いのか、投資主導経済の行方
を探る ─」（10月2日）をご覧いただきたい。 
（https://www.mizuhobank.co.jp/corporate/mhri/research/report/pdf/outlook261002.pdf）

実質ＧＤＰ成長率の推移

（資料）内閣府経済社会総合研究所「四半期別ＧＤＰ速報」

(注）2602までは当研究所による改定見込値、2603は予測値
予測

１. 11/16に内閣府から公表される 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDPは、前期比▲0.1％（前期比年
率▲0.3％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長になったと推計される。 
２. 高水準の企業収益を背景に設備投資が前期比 1.4％と大幅に増加したが、天候不順の影響
などから民間消費が前期比▲0.3％と減少したこと、原油を中心に輸入が大幅に増加し、
外需が成長率を大きく押し下げたことなどから、小幅ながら 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成
長になったとみられる。 
３. 名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質GDPの伸
びを大きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％、前年比 2.5％と予測する。 
４. 2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時
的な影響が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回
復を妨げる可能性が高い。 
５. 現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むこ
となどから、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまる
と予想している。 
ニッセイ基礎研究所 2026-10-30

2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP 
～前期比▲0.1％（年率▲0.3％）を予測～

経済研究部 経済調査部長 斎藤  太郎 
(03)3512-1836  tsaito@nli-research.co.jp

### Page 2

名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率 2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質 GDPの伸びを大
きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％（4-6月期：同 1.0％）、前年比 2.5％（4-6月
期：同 2.6％）と予測する。

なお、11/16に内閣府から 2026年 7-9月期の GDP速報が発表される際には、基礎統計の改定や季
節調整のかけ直しなどから、成長率が過去に遡って改定される。当研究所では、2026年 4-6月期の
実質 GDP成長率は、外需、設備投資が上方修正されることから、前期比年率 1.4％から同 2.3％へ
上方修正されると予想している。

2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時的な影響
が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回復を妨げる可能性が
高い。消費者物価上昇率は 2026年 9月に 9ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となったが、原材料価格の上昇を耐久
財や日用品などの販売価格に転嫁する動きが今後さらに広がることから、2026年度末にかけて 3％
台まで高まる公算が大きい。物価上昇率の高まりによる実質購買力の低下を背景に 2026年度後半
の民間消費は弱めの動きが続くことが予想される。 
現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むことなどか
ら、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまると予想している。

1 10/30までに公表された経済指標をもとに予測している。今後公表される経済指標の結果によって予測値を修正する可
能性がある。

### Page 3

民間消費は前期比▲0.3％と予測する。 
実質賃金はプラスの伸びが続いているが、物価上昇率の高まりによって実質購買力が押し下げら
れていることに加え、台風や豪雨、長雨などの天候不順が個人消費を下押しした。 
2026年 7-9月期の消費関連指標を確認すると、延べ宿泊者数（うち日本人）は前期比 2.8％（4-6
月期：同▲0.9％）の増加となったが、自動車税の
「環境性能割」の廃止を受けて 4-6月期に前期比
10.7％の高い伸びとなった自動車販売台数が、その
反動で同▲7.5％と落ち込んだほか、外食産業売上
高が前期比▲0.3％（4-6月期：同 0.2％）、小売業
販売額指数が前期比▲2.1％（4-6月期：同 0.6％）
と減少した（いずれもニッセイ基礎研究所による
季節調整値、外食産業売上高、小売業販売額指数
は消費者物価指数で実質化）。

・住宅投資～弱めの動きが続く～

住宅投資は前期比▲0.2％と 2四半期連続の減
少を予測する。 
新設住宅着工戸数（季節調整済・年率換算値）
は、建築物省エネ法・建築基準法改正前の駆け
込み需要の反動で、2025年 1-3月期の 86.7万戸
から 4-6月期に 62.0万戸へ急減した後、7-9月
が 71.9万戸と持ち直し、その後は概ね 70万戸
台半ばで推移している。 
GDP統計の住宅投資は住宅価格上昇の影響も
あり弱めの動きとなっている。

・民間設備投資～高水準の企業収益を背景に増加～

民間設備投資は前期比 1.4％と 3四半期ぶりの増加を予測する。 
設備投資の一致指標である資本財総供給は 2026年 4-6月期の前期比 4.9％の後、7-9月期も同 4.9％
となった。また、機械投資の先行指標である機械受注（船舶・電力を除く民需）は 2026年 4-6月
期に前期比 0.2％と 3四半期連続で増加した後、7、8月の平均は 4-6月期を 0.4％上回っている。 
日銀短観 2026年 9月調査では、2026年度の設備投資計画（含むソフトウェア・研究開発投資額、
除く土地投資額）が6月調査から 0.5％上方修正され、前年度比9.3％（全規模・全産業）となった。 
設備投資は、高水準の企業収益を背景に、人手不足対応の省力化投資、デジタル化に向けた情報

・公的固定資本形成～2四半期連続の減少～

公的固定資本形成は、前期比▲2.4％と予測する。 
公共工事の先行指標である公共工事請負金額
は 2026年 7-9月期に前年比▲2.6％と 6四半期ぶ
りに減少した。また、公共工事の進捗を反映する
公共工事出来高（建設総合統計）は、2026年 4-6
月期に前年比 5.2％と 9四半期連続で増加した後、
2026年 7、8月の平均は同 2.4％と伸びが鈍化し
ている。 
2025年度補正予算による押し上げ効果が弱ま
っていることに加え、2026 年度当初予算の公共
事業関係費の伸びが抑えられていることから、弱
めの動きとなっている。

・外需～4四半期ぶりのマイナス寄与～

外需寄与度は前期比▲0.4％（前期比年率▲1.8％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナスを予測する。財貨・
サービスの輸出が前期比 2.0％、財貨・サービスの輸入が同 4.8％となるだろう。代替調達の進展に
伴う原油輸入量の急増を主因として 輸入が高い伸
びとなり、輸出の伸びを上回ったことから、外需は
成長率の押し下げ要因となった。 
2026年 7-9月期の地域別輸出数量指数を季節調整
値（当研究所による試算値）でみると、米国向けが
前期比 0.9％（4-6月期：同 2.8％） 、EU向けが前期
比▲2.4％（4-6月期：同 0.3％） 、アジア向けが前期
比▲0.3％（4-6 月期：同▲0.8％） 、うち中国向けが

2026年 10月 30日 全8頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 9月雇用統計 
失業率は2.4％と前月から0.1％pt低下し、就業拡大が進展

経済調査部 エコノミスト 菊池 慈陽

[要約] 
◼ 2026年 9月の完全失業率（季節調整値）は 2.4％と、2カ月ぶりに低下（前月差▲
0.1％pt）し、7月の水準に戻った。失業者数は「自発的な離職」を中心に 2カ月ぶり
に減少（同▲10万人）し、就業者数は2カ月連続で増加（同＋34万人）した。労働参
加と就業拡大が進展し、雇用環境は前月から改善したとみられる。 
◼ 2026年9月の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は1.19倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と3カ月ぶり
に上昇した。新規求職申込件数が大きく減少したことで、新規求人倍率は2.19倍（同
＋0.13pt）と上昇した。 
◼ 先行きの雇用環境は堅調に推移しよう。労働供給が中長期的に減少していく可能性が
高いこともあり、企業は高水準の賃上げなど、人材確保に向けた積極的な取り組みを
続けている。ただし、雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない。中東情勢をめぐる不確実
性は依然として高く、長期化する原油高が企業収益を悪化させ、幅広い産業で労働需
要を下押しする恐れがある。

### Page 2

2 / 8

9月の完全失業率：2.4％と前月から低下、「自発的な離職」による失業者が大きく減少 
2026年 9月の完全失業率（季節調整値）は2.4％と、2カ月ぶりに低下（前月差▲0.1％pt）
し、7月の水準に戻った（図表2左上） 。失業者数は2カ月ぶりに減少（同▲10万人）し、就業
者数は 2カ月連続で増加（同＋34万人）した。労働参加と就業拡大が進展し、雇用環境は前月
から改善したとみられる。 
失業者数を求職理由別に見ると、「自発的な離職」 （前月差▲13万人）が大きく減少し、全体
を押し下げた。「勤め先や事業の都合による離職」（同▲1万人）は小幅に減少した。他方、「定
年又は雇用契約の満了」（同＋1万人）と「新たに求職」（同＋1万人）は小幅に増加した（巻末
の雇用概況①下段左）。 
就業者数の前月からの変化を業種別に見ると、「製造業」と「医療，福祉」が大きく増加し、
全体を押し上げた。「卸売業，小売業」と「情報通信業」も増加した。他方、「建設業」やその
他の非製造業は減少した。（図表2左下）。 
雇用者数（役員を除く）を雇用形態別に見ると、正規雇用者は前月から 22万人増加した。
2026年春頃から増加傾向にあり、ピークだった2025年 11月の水準を上回った。非正規雇用者
は 5カ月ぶりに増加（前月差＋21万人）した（図表 2右下）。

9月の新規求人倍率：求職が大きく減少した一方で求人は増加し、前月から上昇 
2026年 9月の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は 1.19倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と 3カ月ぶりに上
昇した。新規求人倍率は2.19倍（同＋0.13pt）と上昇した（図表3左）。 
求人側の動きを見ると、有効求人数は前月から横ばいだった一方、新規求人数（前月比＋
1.5％）は 2カ月ぶりに増加した（図表 4左） 。求職側では、有効求職者数（同▲1.4％）、新規
求職申込件数（同▲4.4％）はいずれも 3カ月ぶりに減少した。求職側は 2025年春頃から持ち
直しの動きが見られており、求人側も2026年春頃から増加傾向にある。 
雇用形態別に見ると、正社員の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は1.01倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と
3カ月ぶりに上昇した。新規求人倍率も 1.79倍（同＋0.11pt）と 3カ月ぶりに上昇した（図表
3右）。 
図表 3：有効求人倍率と新規求人倍率（左：全数、右：正社員）

先行き：堅調な推移を見込むも、中東情勢など雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない 
先行きの雇用環境は、堅調に推移することを見込んでいる。労働供給が中長期的に減少して
いく可能性が高いこともあり、企業は高水準の賃上げなど1、人材確保に向けた積極的な取り組
みを続けている。 
日本銀行「全国企業短期経済観測調査」 （日銀短観）の2026年 9月調査では、雇用人員判断
DI（全規模全産業、 「過剰」－「不足」 ）は「最近」が▲38％ptと 6月調査から 1％pt低下し
（人手不足感が強まり）、「先行き」は▲41％ptと更なる低下が予想されている（巻末の雇用概
況①上段右）。特に、中小企業（全産業）は「最近」が▲42％ptと6月調査から3％pt低下し、
「先行き」は▲45％ptだった。中小企業を中心に、人手不足感が強まっているとみられる。 
株式会社ナウキャストによる「HRog賃金 Now」の求人数指数を見ると、正社員とパート・ア
ルバイトのいずれも 2026年春頃から上昇傾向にある（巻末の雇用概況③下段左）。人手不足感
の強まりを受けて、今後も労働需要は堅調に推移するとみられる。 
他方、トランプ米政権による高関税政策（トランプ関税）1F
2や、長期化する日中関係の冷え込
みは、引き続き労働需要の下押し要因となるだろう。 
また、雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない。中東情勢は依然として不確実性が高い状況が続
いており、とりわけ注意が必要だ。原油等の供給網の正常化は当面見込みにくく、輸入原油価
格が高止まりしている。原油高は原材料費や物流費などの上昇を通じて企業収益を悪化させ3、
幅広い産業で労働需要を下押しする可能性がある。

1 日本労働組合総連合会（連合）が公表した第 7回（最終）回答集計結果では、定期昇給相当込みの賃上げ率
（加重平均）が企業規模計で 5.01％と、前年（5.25％）をやや下回るものの、高水準を維持している。300人
未満の中小組合では 4.69％と前年（4.65％）をわずかながら上回り、中小企業の積極的な姿勢が目立った。詳
細は、日本労働組合総連合会（連合）「3年連続の 5％超え！～2026春季生活闘争 第7回（最終）回答集計結
果について～」（2026年7月3日）を参照。 
2 トランプ米政権は通商法 122条に基づく措置が失効する 7月 24日に新たな関税措置を発動した（日本経済新
聞 電子版「トランプ政権が新関税発動、日本 12.5％ 中間選挙へ看板政策継続」（2026年7月 24日））。 
3 原油高による物価の上昇については、神田慶司・畑中宏仁・中村華奈子・横田凱「日本経済見通し：2026年
8月」（大和総研レポート、2026年8月 24日）を参照。

Greater China Technology Hardware  | Asia Pacific
2026 TPCA Show Takeaways
In our PCB supply chain coverage, our order of preference is:
TUC, EMC, Co-Tech, UMTC, NYPCB, and GCE, based on upside to
our price targets.

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
Implications to our coverage: If next-gen AI server racks do not adopt PTFE-based
CCL, we think the outcome would skew positive for EMC and TUC but slightly

negative for Shengyi. We expect the material choice to be confirmed by mid-2027 at
the latest. Additionally, with EMC expecting its substrate CCL capacity to more than
double by end-2028, Unimicron and other substrate makers gain an alternative
supply source, which we view as positive. Substrate CCL carries higher gross
margins than PCB CCL, so a higher contribution from this business should improve
EMC’s profitability as S572T ramps from 4Q26. On ABF substrate, lead times of at

least two years for key equipment such as EPM’s ABF laminators imply that new
greenfield ABF substrate capacity can only ramp from 2029 onwards. This aligns
with our view that ABF substrate undersupply will deepen in 2027-28. Substrate
sizes going to 230mm x 230mm by 2028, implying only 4 substrates per panel, is
also supportive of our positive view on demand. This would make it very difficult to
control yield, as one area of defect on the panel would imply 25% yield loss.

PTFE CCL: Adoption of glass cloth-free PTFE CCLs in next-gen AI server systems
was the topic investors asked about most at the booths. As we have written
previously (here and here), pure PTFE CCLs could deliver the best signal integrity
(SI), making them the preferred solution for midplane/switch PCBs for some
customers. However, their processability at PCB makers remains a challenge for
now, particularly for high-layer-count boards. In addition, multilayer boards (MLBs)

using PTFE require special high-temperature presses capable of reaching close to
400°C, as standard vacuum presses are insufficient. In response, Taiwanese CCL
suppliers are also exploring other materials that could deliver M10-and-beyond
performance, including hydrocarbon (HC)-based, HC+PTFE hybrid and
benzocyclobutene (BCB)-based CCLs. We expect the CCL material for the next-gen
AI server rack to be confirmed by mid-2027 at the latest.
Other key takeaways can be found on the following page.

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
PTFE offers the best SI but is harder to process; TW CCL makers are less bullish
on PTFE and are also exploring HC, HC+PTFE hybrid & BCB.
Thin E-glass cloth shortages and price hikes to last through 1H27, at least, as
looms stay scarce and lines shift to LDK/T-glass.
EPM’s ABF laminators (95%+ global share) are a key bottleneck to ABF substrate

expansion, with 2+ year lead times and no new greenfield capacity until 2029.
Glass core substrate is likely not commercially viable in the next 2-3 years, with
crack inspection after ABF lamination currently being problematic.
October 26, 2026 01:42 PM GMT

### Page 2

M
Idea
2
Substrate CCL: S572T, the most advanced substrate CCL on display at EMC’s booth,
enters mass production in 4Q26, with volumes expected to increase sequentially
throughout 2027. EMC expects its substrate CCL capacity to more than double from
current levels by end-2028 and said it will have multiple major ABF substrate
customers. It added that it has qualified additional low-CTE glass sources to ensure
sufficient supply.
Glass cloth shortage: Several CCL suppliers still cite thin E-glass cloth as the key

material bottleneck. They generally expect the shortage to persist through 1H27
with continued price hikes, and one supplier believes it could last through late 2027.
In their view, the tightness in electronic-grade glass cloth stems from insufficient
supply of Japanese-made looms, as well as from existing glass cloth suppliers either
prioritizing in-house demand or converting production lines to LDK1/2 and T-glass
cloth to meet strong AI demand. One glass cloth supplier noted it remains reluctant

to adopt non-Toyota looms given the higher defect rates and repair costs.
ABF laminator: We also visited the booth of Eternal Materials (1717.TW, Not
Covered), whose subsidiary Eternal Precision Mechanics (EPM, 7795.TW, Not
Covered) holds 95%+ global market share in vacuum laminators used to laminate
ABF onto substrate cores. EPM’s ABF laminators are now one of the key
bottlenecks in ABF substrate capacity expansion. Our checks with EPM suggest
equipment lead times have stretched to at least two years and EPM’s new

greenfield capacity will not come online until 2029; until then, EPM can only
debottleneck by optimizing existing capacity and leasing idle capacity from third
parties. For context, a new ABF substrate plant may require at least 10 sets of EPM’s
most advanced three-stage vacuum laminator and pre-track machine, depending on
factory layout.
Glass core substrate: Microcracks and the resulting package reliability issues are
currently the two key technical bottlenecks for glass core substrates. Existing

automated optical inspection (AOI) tools struggle to detect and identify microcracks
in the glass core, and laminating multiple layers of Ajinomoto Build-up Film (ABF)
onto it makes inspection impossible, according to Shinko. One equipment supplier
also flagged metallization of high-aspect-ratio through-glass vias (TGVs) as very
challenging. We understand this supplier is not involved in copper pillar mass
transfer, an alternative approach to TGV metallization.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

### Page 1

Summary: Our first take on Unimicron’s 3Q26 results (released on 27 October) is that 
earnings were better than expected, driven by stronger-than-expected non-operating 
gains. We attribute the upside primarily to a smaller-than-feared valuation loss on UMC in 
3Q26. In addition, the company mentioned that other investments, including Unimicron 
shares held through investment vehicles, contributed c.TWD3bn of mark-to-market gains in 
3Q26. As for the core business, 3Q26 revenue reached TWD52.7bn (up 23% QoQ, up 
55% YoY), with gross margin expanding 4.0ppt QoQ to 28.8% and operating margin rising 
4.5ppt QoQ to 20.0%, supported by improving profitability in ABF substrates and HDI. We 
believe the profitability performance was broadly in line with the latest buy-side 
expectations. Looking ahead, while the company does not provide numerical guidance, 
revenue and gross margin should continue to improve in 4Q26. Given Unimicron’s leading 
position across most CSP AI ASIC projects, Nvidia GPUs, and EMIB-T, we believe the 
company should continue to benefit from a favourable pricing environment amid the 
current supply-demand dynamics and ongoing specification upgrades, including larger 
substrate body sizes and technology migration. We have a Buy (1) rating on Unimicron 
with a 12M TP of TWD1,500, based on a target PER of 35x applied to our 1-year-forward 
EPS forecast. Key downside risk: weaker-than-expected demand.

What’s the impact: 
Below is our first take on Unimicron’s 3Q26 results (released on 27 October). We will 
review our model in due course.

• 3Q26 results beat expectations. Unimicron’s 3Q26 earnings came in at TWD10.1bn 
(EPS: TWD6.15), beating our estimate of TWD4.2bn and Bloomberg consensus of 
TWD6.7bn by 140% and 49%, respectively, mainly due to higher-than-expected non-
operating gains. In our previous note, Unimicron recognised c.TWD8bn of mark-to-
market gains on UMC (2303 TT, TWD122.5, Neutral [3], covered by Rick Hsu) in 2Q26. 
We had originally expected Unimicron to recognise c.TWD5bn of valuation losses in 
3Q26, given UMC’s share-price performance in July. However, the actual valuation loss 
of c.TWD0.7bn was substantially better than feared. In addition, the company attributed 
part of the strength to c.TWD3bn of other mark-to-market gains, including gains related 
to Unimicron shares held through its investment vehicles. This is broadly consistent with 
the reported 3Q26 total non-operating contribution of c.TWD2.6bn after accounting for 
the UMC valuation loss and other non-operating items.

### Page 2

Unimicron Technology Corp (3037 TT) 
27 October 2026 
2

• Looking at the core business, 3Q26 revenue reached TWD52.7bn (up 23% QoQ, up 
55% YoY), with gross margin expanding 4.0ppt QoQ to 28.8% and operating margin 
rising 4.5ppt QoQ to 20.0%, supported by improving profitability in ABF substrates and 
HDI. With stronger operating leverage, operating profit surged 58% QoQ to 
TWD10.53bn. We believe the gross-margin and operating-margin performance was 
broadly in line with recent buy-side expectations for modestly improving profitability in 
ABF substrates and HDI, given that ABF margin was already high at c.40% in 2Q26, 
alongside a higher revenue contribution from a large end customer. We estimate ABF 
substrate margin at c.42-44% in 3Q26 (vs. c.40% in 2Q26).

• Business outlook. While the company does not provide numerical guidance, revenue 
and gross margin should continue to improve in 4Q26 amid the current substrate supply 
shortage. In addition, the company is aggressively expanding capacity at its Guangfu 
and Yangmei plants over 2027-29, with roughly c.30% annual ABF capacity expansion, 
which should become a key growth driver for Unimicron. Given Unimicron’s leading 
position across most CSP AI ASIC projects, Nvidia GPUs, and EMIB-T, we believe the 
company should continue to benefit from a favourable pricing environment amid the 
current supply-demand dynamics and ongoing specification upgrades, including larger 
substrate body sizes and technology migration.

What we recommend: 
We have a Buy (1) rating on Unimicron with a 12M TP of TWD1,500, based on a target 
PER of 35x applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Key downside risk: less-than-
expected demand.

In Taiwan, we prefer TUC over EMC for greater pricing upside in
the next 12 months, given its higher exposure to midrange/low-
end CCL. Both stocks are attractively valued: EMC at 11x CY28e
P/E and TUC at 9x.
EMC (2383.TW)
• EMC has introduced a new expansion plan for its total CCL capacity to reach
11.95mn sheets per month (SPM) in 2029 and 15.25mn in 2030 (+14% and

+28% y/y). See Exhibit 3  for EMC's 2026-2030 capacity plans.
• EMC's substrate CCL capacity currently sits at 300K SPM. The company
expects to add another 900K SPM in 2H27, reaching 1.2M SPM by end-2027.
Our supply chain checks suggest that a US hyperscaler  will be the first key
customer of EMC's ABF substrate CCLs for its  eighth-generation ASIC (see
our  TPCA takeaways).
• Management noted a growing trend of customers requesting long-term
agreements that span at least three years, on average. These L TAs now

secure volume rather than fixing prices; they do not require prepayments
from customers. Despite the flexibility in pricing and payment, customers
will still face punishment if they fail to pull their promised volumes.
• We estimate that 4Q revenue will grow ~19% q/q, to NT$72.4bn (+190% y/y),
supported by pricing adjustments and continued mix improvement, given
limited capacity increase in 4Q. We see GM expanding 220bps q/q, to 39.9%,
for EPS of NT$53.33 (+32% q/q).

TUC (6274.TWO)
• Management expects price hikes to continue into 4Q, with new pricing for its
M7/8 CCL taking effect from November onwards. For M6 and below, pricing
will remain driven by raw material cost pass-through amid supply tightness.
We expect this trend to persist into 2027, with HVLP4 copper foil, E glass,
and LDK2 glass cloth to be the most undersupplied.
• We believe recent market concerns about TUC's "share loss" for an ASIC
customer are overdone. Given the ongoing supply tightness in the high-end

CCL market, it is normal for CSPs to adopt multi-sourcing strategies to
diversify supply risks. Meanwhile, we believe TUC is actively engaging and
sampling high-end M8+ grade materials with other major CSP customer(s)
for next-gen AI projects. TUC's share in a key satellite customer's next-gen
satellite products will potentially expand in 2027 as well, with materials
upgraded to M7/8.
• We expect 4Q revenue to grow 15% q/q, to NT$20.6bn (+126% y/y), driven

by pricing adjustments and capacity addition at TUC's site in Thailand site
(+100K SPM in 4Q, +200K SPM in 3Q). We see GM expanding 50bps q/q, to
37.4%, for EPS of NT$15.82 (+20% q/q).

Price Target Discussion and Valuation Methodology
Our price target for EMC increases ~5%, to NT$8,800, driven by increases in our

2026-28 earnings estimates: Our new price target implies 20x CY28e P/E (unchanged).
Our price target is also our base case scenario value. It is derived from our residual income
(RI) model (similar to the rest of our tech hardware coverage).
To calculate the value of the stock, we use the company's beginning equity plus the
present value of all expected residual income of earnings in excess of cost of capital. The
RI is positive when ROAE is above the cost of capital and negative when it is below the

cost of capital. Our key assumptions (all unchanged) include a cost of equity of 9.3% (beta
of 1.0, equity risk premium of 8.7%, and risk-free rate of 1.0%), a medium-term growth rate
of 12%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
The 20x CY28 P/E implied by our price target is slightly above the past three years'
average +1SD of 18x, but much lower than the 32x recent P/E peak. We deem this
valuation fair because EMC is benefiting from an earnings growth cycle fueled by stronger

AI server CCL demand, EMC's own mix improvement, and continued pricing adjustments,
which should support margin expansion.
Our bull and bear case values also increase ~5%, to NT$13,650 and NT$4,200,
respectively.

TUC: Earnings Estimate Changes
We raise our  gross margin and operating margin assumptions for CY26-28 to factor in the
margin beat in 3Q26. However, we also revise our tax rate assumptions from 4Q26
onwards to reflect the higher-than-expected tax rate in 3Q26 (31.1% vs. our 25.0%
estimate). Our  EPS estimates consequently change +3% for CY26, -5% for CY27, and -6%
for CY28.

Price target discussion and valuation methodology
Our price target for TUC decreases  ~6%, to NT$2,550, primarily driven by the changes
in our earnings estimates for CY27-28: This implies 28x CY27e or 17x CY28e P/E. Our
price target is also our base case scenario value. It is derived from our residual income (RI)
model (similar to the rest of our tech hardware coverage).
To calculate the value of the stock, we use the company's beginning equity plus the

present value of all expected residual income of earnings in excess of cost of capital. The
RI is positive when ROAE is above the cost of capital and negative when it is below the
cost of capital. Our key assumptions (all unchanged) include a cost of equity of 9.3% (beta
of 1.0, equity risk premium of 8.7%, and risk-free rate of 1.0%), a medium-term growth rate
of 11%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
The 17x CY28e P/E implied by our price target is roughly in line with the past three years'

average +1SD of 17.8x. We deem this valuation fair because TUC is benefiting from an
earnings growth cycle fueled by stronger AI server/networking CCL demand, TUC's own
mix improvement, and continued pricing adjustments, which should support margin
expansion.
Our bull and bear case scenario values also decline ~6%, to NT$3,825 and NT$635,
respectively.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

读懂 8 月工业企业盈利的结构性修复 ，
2026 年9 月28 日

如何看待利润增长放缓下的行业“温差”？
2026 年8 月27 日

企业利润延续两位数增速，下半年分化仍
是主要特征，2026 年7 月27 日

价格与利润率支撑工业企业利润高增，电
子行业贡献超四成，2026 年6 月29 日

工业企业利润延续改善，上下游分化进一
步加剧，2026 年5 月27 日

价格上涨支撑工业企业利润延续改善，原
材料和新动能表现亮眼，2026 年4 月27 日

价格结构性抬升下行业利润分化或加剧 
             ——9 月工业企业利润数据点评

事件：1-9 月工业企业营收同比增长 6.6%，较去年同期提升
4.2 个百分点，与今年 1-8 月持平；1-9 月工业企业利润同比增长
15.5%，较去年同期提升12.3 个百分点、较今年1-8 月下降0.2 个
百分点，其中 9 月当月利润同比增长 14.4%，较去年同期下降 7.2
个百分点，较今年8 月提升10.2 个百分点。 
量价同时改善，营收利润率小幅抬升，共同支撑 9 月利润增速
边际回升。1-9 月工业企业利润同比增长 15.5%，其中 9 月工业企
业利润同比增长 14.4%，较上月回升 10.2 个百分点。营收方面，
1-9 月工业企业营收同比增长6.6%，与1-8 月持平。其中，量的方
面，外需强劲支撑工业生产，9 月出口交货值同比增长 17%，较上
月提升 5.9 个百分点，带动 9 月工业增加值同比增长 5.5%，较上
月提升 0.3 个百分点。价的方面，受中东局势紧张、AI 产业快速
发展、新能源相关需求抬升等因素影响，国际原油、有色等大宗
商品价格上行，带动9 月PPI 同比增长5%，较上月大幅提升1.2 个
百分点。营收利润率方面，1-9 月营业收入利润率为 5.68%，我们
测算 9 月单月营业收入利润率为 5.81%、较上月上升 0.14 个百分
点，结合成本费用来看，9 月单月成本率较上月下降 0.89 个百分
点至84.65%、费用率较上月上升0.4 个百分点至8.35%，整体来看
9 月企业盈利能力较上月稍有改善。
工业企业利润数据点评 2026 年10 月27 日   2026 年第59 期

赵 耿 010-66428731

www.ccxi.com.cn 
9 月工业企业利润数据点评： 
价格结构性抬升下行业利润分化或加剧 
企业产成品库存与上月基本持平，应收账款回收期略有缩短 。9 月工业企业产成品存
货同比增长 11.6%，较上月上升 0.6 个百分点，从金额来看，9 月产成品存款 7.37 万亿，
与上月基本持平，但与9 月制造业PMI 数据背离，9 月制造业PMI 产成品库存分项为47.6%、
较上月下降 0.8 个百分点，可能因为制造业 PMI 产成品库存反映的是产成品数量的变化，
而工业企业产成品存货反映的是金额的变化，二者背离说明产成品存货单位成本的上升，
与近期 PPI 走强表现一致。9 月工业企业应收账款余额同比增长 10%，较 1-8 月上升 1 个
百分点，而应收账款回收期较 8 月下滑 0.6 天至 71.6 天，主要是因为 9 月营业收入环比
增速（7.89%）高于应收账款余额环比增速（0.97%），表明 9 月工业企业现销收入增加，
以及应收账款回收力度加大，与9 月企业活期存款增加相一致。 
国有企业利润增速边际抬升，私营企业利润增速边际下滑。1-9 月国企利润同比增长
13.1%，较 1-8 月上升 2.8 个百分点，原油等大宗商品价格上行或是主要原因；私营企业
利润同比增长 8.7%，较 1-8 月下降 1.7 个百分点，今年整体呈“前高后低”态势；股份
制企业利润同比增长20.8%，较1-8 月小幅下降0.4 个百分点；高基数下外商及港澳台企
业利润同比增长0.5%，较1-8 月下降 1.8 个百分点。

9 月工业企业利润数据点评： 
价格结构性抬升下行业利润分化或加剧 
行业利润分化加剧， 超六成行业利润负增长， 原油与有色相关产业链和电子产业链
利润持续高增，消费品制造业盈利修复仍需时日。1-9 月采矿业利润同比增长 38.2%，较
1-8 月抬升 3.1 个百分点，利润延续高增。国际原油价格高位运行，以及 AI、新能源相关
产业链对有色金属的需求旺盛是主要原因，1-9 月，石油开采和有色金属采选业利润分别
同比增长 21.3%和 68%。1-9 月制造业利润同比增长 17%，较 1-8 月微降0.4 个百分点，原
材料制造业和高技术制造业表现亮眼，1-9 月，原材料制造业利润同比增长 44.9%，其中，
原油相关的化工、化纤制造行业利润分别同比增长 49.2%和 81.7%，有色金属加工业利润
同比增长 79.7%；高技术制造业利润同比增长 52%，其中电子行业利润同比增长 1.0 倍，
拉动全部规模以上工业企业利润增长 9.6 个百分点。但是，终端消费需求疲弱下，消费制
造业利润持续负增长，1-9 月，汽车、农副食品加工、纺织服装、家具制造等行业利润分
别同比下滑 22.8%、17.3%、15.6%和 51.3%。1-9 月公共事业利润同比下滑 14.3%，主要源
于电力、热力生产和供应业利润同比下滑 17.9%，今年以来跌幅持续扩大，煤炭价格上行
抬升生产成本，电力市场化改革推动电价下降，共同挤压电力行业利润。 
与去年同期相比，中游行业利润占比相对稳定，上游、下游行业利润占比一升一降。
1-9 月，上、中、下游行业利润占比
1
分别为 26.26%、50.8%和 22.94%，与上月相比变化不

9 月工业企业利润数据点评： 
价格结构性抬升下行业利润分化或加剧 
工、化纤制造行业利润占比也有小幅提升，而其余行业如金属 /非金属制造、通用/专用/
运输设备制造等行业利润占比则较去年同期有不同程度下降。 
整体来看，在工业生产加快、价格上行带动下，9 月工业企业利润回升至两位数增速。
展望后续，四季度行业利润 或延续改善趋势，但 分化延续，利润仍将向原油、有色金属
以及 AI 相关产业集中。其一，中东冲突局势未定，近来国际原油价格持续维持在 100 美
元/桶的高位，利好石油开采及其相关的化工化纤行业利润增长，同时，冬季采暖叠加对
原油的替代需求增加，煤炭行业利润也将延续亮眼表现。其二，AI、新能源产业快速发展，
对铜、铝、锂等有色金属的需求旺盛，支撑有色金属矿采和加工行业利润。其三， AI 投
资热度未减，相关产品价格上涨显著，需求的快速增长带动先进存储、电子电路制造等电
子行业利润快速增长，同时，算力与数据中心建设带动光纤制造、光缆制造等行业利润抬

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -2.62 điểm tương ứng -0.15%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Công nghệ và Máy móc là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -13,987 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã HDB, PNJ và VJC; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-30 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,267 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -2,363 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 3,726 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -2,702 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua một tuần đi ngang giằng co, giảm nhẹ và đóng cửa ở 1.735 điểm 
với thanh khoản ở mức thấp, và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài bán ròng cả 5 phiên trong tuần. 
Thông tin vĩ mô tích cực với tăng trưởng GDP quý 3 đạt mức gần 10% là điểm sáng trong 
tuần qua, tuy đã không đủ nâng đỡ thị trường chung. Thay vào đó, tâm lý thị trường thận 
trọng trước số liệu lạm phát ở mức cao, đợt tăng giá xăng dầu trong nước và mặt bằng lãi 
suất trong nước chưa có dấu hiệu hạ nhiệt. Diễn biến tăng của giá dầu thế giới, và lợi suất 
trái phiếu Chính phủ Mỹ cũng là các yếu tố gây áp lực lên thị trường chung. Tâm lý thận 
trọng còn thể hiện qua áp lực bán giải chấp ở một số cổ phiếu riêng lẻ như PNJ, SHB..., dù 
một vài nhóm trụ như dầu khí, tiêu dùng có thời điểm đóng vai trò nâng đỡ chỉ số.

Trần Đức Anh 
Giám đốc Kinh tế Vĩ mô & Chiến lược thị trường 
anhtd@kbsec.com.vn 
07/10/2026

Triển vọng TTCK 4Q2026 
Tích lũy trong vùng nhiễu động

Chúng tôi dự báo vùng điểm hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm 2026 
xuống 1,800 đi ểm, giảm mạnh từ mức 1,950 đi ểm đưa ra trong báo cáo gần nhất. 
Vùng điểm này tương ứng:

- Tăng trưởng EPS 2026 so với cùng kỳ của thị trường chung ở mức 33% 
(tăng mạnh so với mức 25% của báo cáo gần nhất), chủ yếu nhờ kỳ vọng 
vào các chính sách thúc đẩy kinh tế của Chính phủ, và lợi nhuận tăng 
trưởng đột biến ở VIC (xem thêm phần Triển vọng hoạt động kinh doanh).

- Mức P/E hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm ở mức 13.2 (giảm 
nhẹ so với mức 13.5 thời điểm hiện tại - phản ánh quan điểm các yếu tố 
rủi ro sẽ tiếp tục duy trì). Đây là mức định giá thấp so với bình quân 5 năm 
gần nhất ở 15.1. Nếu loại bỏ yếu tố đột biến ở nhóm Vingroup, chỉ số P/E 
điều chỉnh hiện đã rơi xuống 10.9, thấp hơn các đáy gần nhất năm 2020, 
2022 và 2025. Trong bối cảnh kinh tế Việt Nam duy trì tốc độ tăng trưởng 
cao, lợi nhuận các doanh nghiệp niêm yết liên tục bứt phá, vùng định giá 
này mở ra cơ hội đầu tư trung/dài hạn. Chúng tôi cho rằng ngay khi các rủi 
ro vĩ mô quốc tế (xoay quanh vấn đề Iran), và trong nước (xoay quanh vấn 
đề lãi suất) hạ nhiệt, thị trường chung sẽ có cơ hội tái định giá lên mức cao 
hơn đáng kể so với thời điểm hiện tại. Dù vậy, khác với báo cáo gần nhất 
vào tháng 7, ở báo cáo này, chúng tôi đẩy lui thời điểm các yếu tố rủi ro vĩ 
mô hạ nhiệt sang năm 2027, đồng nghĩa với quan điểm thận trọng hơn về 
thị trường cho quý 4 2026.

Cụ thể, đối với vấn đề lãi suất, áp lực lạm phát lớn dần, đi kèm nhu cầu vốn cao phục 
vụ hoạt động đầu tư cơ sở hạ tầng sẽ khiến mặt bằng lãi suất duy trì ở mức cao cho 
phần còn lại của năm 2026 (dù thanh khoản hệ thống ghi nhận sự cải thiện đáng kể 
trong 1 quý gần đây). Đối với vấn đề xung đột Trung Đông, các cuộc đàm phán vẫn 
đang bế tắc và ít có kh ả năng dòng ch ảy dầu mỏ tại khu vực này được nối lại ngay 
trong năm nay.

Chủ đề đầu tư chúng tôi l ựa chọn cho 4Q2026 là đ ầu tư công, chính sách ti ền tệ hỗ 
trợ, và xung đột Iran.

Xem đầy đủ tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành mẫu nến Doji trên khung đồ thị tuần sau hai tuần giảm điểm liên tiếp, 
cho thấy áp lực bán đã phần nào được kiểm soát. Trên khung ngày, nỗ lực hồi phục đầu 
tuần gặp lực cản tại đường SMA50 khiến VNINDEX quay đầu giảm điểm, tiếp tục chớm phá 
đáy ngắn hạn; tuy nhiên vùng hỗ trợ gần đã tạo hiệu ứng thu hút lực cầu bắt đáy, giúp chỉ 
số thu hẹp đà giảm trong phiên cuối tuần. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh khá tiêu cực từ vùng đỉnh 
đầu tháng 9, VNINDEX đang bước vào giai đoạn tìm kiếm điểm cân bằng trở lại và diễn biến 
giằng co điều chỉnh có thể còn kéo dài thêm trong các phiên kế tiếp. Tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn tính từ vùng đáy cuối tháng 7 vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ 
vọng sẽ có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại tại điểm đỡ gần quanh 1710 (đây cũng là chốt chặn cần 
giữ để bảo vệ được xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn).

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu 
Mã Giá đóng cửa tuần Biến động từ đầu tháng Giá khuyến nghị mua Giá mục tiêu Lợi nhuận  kỳ vọng Điểm nhấn đầu tư Báo cáo phân tích 
FRT 149.60 1.8% 143.40 165.00 15.1% 
- Sau khi phủ sóng các thành phố lớn, chuỗi Long Châu đang mở rộng sang các vùng cấp 2, 3. 
Năm 2026 dự kiến mở thêm 400-450 cửa hàng (+20%YoY) với DT trên mỗi cửa hàng cũ tăng 
trưởng 6%-8%/năm 
- Dư địa lấy thêm thị phần từ các cửa hàng nhỏ lẻ vẫn còn tương đối rộng, FRT đặt mục tiêu 
chiếm 50% thị phần doanh thu ngành bán lẻ dược phẩm, từ mức 17%-20% hiện tại

TUẦN 21-25/09 [Điểm nhấn thị trường] 
VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tu ần qua, -30.55 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh 
khoản suy giảm

[Cập nhật công ty] 
FPT , REE, POW, AST , BWE

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
Áp lực điều chỉnh vẫn có thể còn tiếp diễn, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn 
chưa bị phá vỡ, VNINDEX đư ợc kỳ vọng sẽ nhận được lực đỡ tại vùng hỗ trợ gần 
quanh 1750, tương ứng vùng hợp lưu quanh SMA50, và có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại

### Page 2

2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -30.55 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Dệt may, Tiện ích khí và Bao bì & đóng gói là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt 
nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị  
-2,865 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã VHM, VPB và VIC; đồng thời, tự doanh đảo chiều bán 
ròng ở mức -146 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại bán ròng -3,351 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua nhưng 
đang duy trì vị thế mua lũy kế 1,650 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 2,822 hợp đồng nhưng 
đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,636 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam bước vào tuần đầu tiên ở vị thế thị trường mới nổi bằng một nhịp điều 
chỉnh, khi VN-Index để mất mốc 1.800 điểm và lùi về đóng cửa quanh 1.785 điểm. Đây là 
phản ứng "mua kỳ vọng, bán thực tế" khá điển hình: ngay trong phiên nâng hạng chính 
thức 21/9, chỉ số mở cửa hưng phấn rồi nhanh chóng đảo chiều do áp lực chốt lời, với 
nhóm cổ phiếu Vingroup (VIC, VHM, VRE) giảm mạnh dẫn dắt đà đi xuống, và sức ép lan 
rộng sang bất động sản, ngân hàng, năng lượng về cuối tuần. Điểm đáng chú ý là dòng 
vốn ngoại đã tiếp tục bán ròng ngay sau cột mốc ngày 21/9, dù đã mua ròng trở lại trong 
tuần trước đó. Bối cảnh bên ngoài cũng kém thuận lợi khi Fed vừa tăng lãi suất và phát đi 
tín hiệu diều hâu, chứng khoán Mỹ điều chỉnh, giá dầu neo cao, và lợi suất trái phiếu 
Chính phủ Mỹ tăng vọt. Trong một, hai tuần tới, bên cạnh các yếu tố ngoại biên, mùa báo 
cáo KQKD quý 3 cũng sẽ bắt đầu tác động lên diễn biến thị trường chung.

Lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ tăng 
trưởng khả quan trong 2Q26 14% 
YoY, với động lực chính đến từ mảng 
dịch vụ CNTT nước ngoài

Mảng CNTT nước ngoài: Lĩnh vực dịch 
vụ IT phân cực mạnh mẽ do tác nhân 
AI (AI Agentic) s ẽ củng c ố xu hư ớng 
đầu tư vào AI

FPT ở vị thế thuận lợi cho phép 
doanh nghiệp đa dạng hóa công cụ 
AI và tối ưu chi phí vận hành

Khuyến ngh ị MUA với giá m ục tiêu 
86,000 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Do không còn h ạch toán FPT Telecom (FOX) là công ty con, d oanh thu 2Q2026 của 
FPT giảm mạnh còn 13,810 tỷ VND (-17% YoY). Lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ không 
bị ảnh hưởng bởi thay đổi hạch toán đạt 2,568 tỷ VND (14% YoY). Mảng dịch vụ CNTT 
tại thị trường nước ngoài tiếp tục là đ ộng lực tăng trư ởng chính, đạt doanh thu và 
LNTT lần lượt là 9,868/1,538 tỷ VND (16.3% /19.3% YoY).

Sự chuyển dịch chi tiêu từ dịch vụ IT truyền thống sang chuyển đổi số và tích hợp AI 
được kỳ vọng sẽ diễn ra ngày m ột mạnh mẽ khi: i) Sự phát triển của năng l ực tính 
toán giúp kéo giảm chi phí token (đơn v ị năng lực tính toán của AI) và thúc đ ẩy nhu 
cầu sử dụng AI của doanh nghiệp; ii) Các mô hình AI mã nguồn mở gia tăng lựa chọn 
cho người dùng sẽ thúc đẩy chi tiêu

Đối với các doanh nghiệp ứng dụng sâu các mô hình AI phục vụ hoạt động kinh doanh 
như FPT , chúng tôi nhận thấy xu hướng tối ưu chi phí tốt hơn nhờ khả năng sử dụng 
các mô hình AI đ ến từ đa dạng nhà cung c ấp; với chi phí trên hi ệu năng tính toán 
được cải thi ện. Trong k ịch b ản cơ s ở, chúng tôi d ự báo biên l ợi nhuận trước thu ế 
mảng CNTT nước ngoài sẽ ổn định ở mức 15.4% nhờ duy trì chi ến lược quản trị chi 
phí hiệu quả qua đó giúp LNTT 2026/2027 đạt 6,198/7,087 tỷ VND (13.3%/14.3% YoY).

Dựa trên định giá FCFF và P/E, triển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các rủi ro có 
thể phát sinh, chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu FPT . Giá mục tiêu là 86,000 
VND/cổ phiếu.

tăng trưởng nhẹ

Sản lượng điện của REE tăng trưởng 
nhẹ nhờ mức tăng của mảng điện gió 
sau khi dự án Duyên Hải V1-4 vận 
hành

Mảng M&E ghi nhận giá trị ký mới cao 
trong 1H.2026, t ạo kỳ vọng hồi phục 
mạnh mẽ doanh thu

Mảng cho thuê văn phòng đứng trước 
cơ hội lớn đóng góp vào tăng trư ởng 
nhờ các điều kiện thuận lợi

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
58,100 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Quý 2/2026 giảm nhẹ, đạt 2,432 tỷ VND (-3% YoY/-2% QoQ), trong khi l ợi 
nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ đạt 632 tỷ VND (+1% YoY/-11.5% QoQ), do doanh thu của 
mảng bất động sản suy giảm. Qua đó, REE hoàn thành lần lượt 40%/47.8% kế hoạch 
doanh thu và LNST công ty mẹ 2026.

Trong Q2.2026, tổng sản lượng phát đi ện của REE tăng nh ẹ, đạt 2.8 tỷ kWh (+2.3% 
YoY) với đóng góp tăng trưởng rõ nét đến từ mảng điện gió khi dự án Duyên Hải V1-4 
bắt đầu vận hành; tuy nhiên  sản lượng thủy điện suy gi ảm do tác đ ộng tiêu c ực từ 
pha thời tiết El Nino, đạt 1.1 tỷ kWh.

Với năng lực chuyên môn được nâng cao, mảng M&E ghi nhận những dấu hiệu phục 
hồi tích cực trong giá trị hợp đồng ký mới trong 1H.2026, 5,865 tỷ VND (cao hơn 53.9% 
so với giá trị ký mới cả năm 2025). KBSV dự phóng doanh thu c ủa mảng M&E sẽ đạt 
4,499/5,190 tỷ VND (+28%/15% YoY) trong 2026/2027.

Hội tụ bởi 3 yếu tố: i) Định hướng phát triển kinh tế Hồ Chí Minh trở thành trung tâm 
tài chính quốc tế; ii) Hạ tầng giao thông ngày một được hoàn thiện và iii) Chi phí thuê 
cạnh tranh so với chất lượng dịch vụ của E. Town 6, KBSV dự phóng doanh thu mảng 
cho thuê văn phòng trong 2026/2027 tăng trưởng +14%/6.2% YoY.

Dựa trên đ ịnh giá SOTP , triển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các r ủi ro có th ể 
phát sinh, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu REE. Giá m ục tiêu là 58,100 
VND/cổ phiếu, cao hơn 28.3% so với mức giá đóng cửa tại ngày 15/09/2026.

Doanh thu 2Q.26 bứt phá nhờ khoản 
thu nhập bất thường của nhiệt điện 
Cà Mau và Vũng Áng

Nhiệt đi ện than duy trì công su ất ở 
mức cao nh ờ nguồn cung nhiên li ệu 
ổn định trong 2H26

Điện khí Đông Nam Bộ (NT1&2 và 
NT3&4) có triển vọng hoạt động 
thuận lợi

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
18,100 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Trong Quý 2/2026, POW ghi nhận Doanh thu và LNST cổ đông công ty mẹ đạt lần lượt 
20,308 tỷ VND (116.1% YoY) và 3,519 tỷ VND (484.6% YoY) chủ yếu nhờ các khoản thu 
nhập từ chênh lệch tiền khí của nhiệt điện Cà Mau và tỷ giá của nhiệt điện Vũng Áng. 
Với kết qu ả trên, POW hoàn thành l ần lư ợt 65%/420% kế hoạch doanh thu và l ợi 
nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ.

Chúng tôi cho r ằng nhà máy nhi ệt than Vũng Áng trong 2026 s ẽ được duy trì công 
suất huy động ở mức cao nhờ: i) Xác suất diễn ra El Nino tăng cao và dự kiến kéo dài 
đến đầu 2027, ii) Nguồn cung than trong nước được bảo đảm từ các đối tác (TKV, TCT 
Đông B ắc) và d ự trữ than d ồi dào c ủa doanh nghi ệp ghi nh ận ở mức cao trong 
Q2/2026.

Dự án đường dây 500kV Mạch 3 đi vào vận hành giúp giảm áp lực cạnh tranh của các 
nhà máy điện tại khu vực phía Nam, qua đó kỳ vọng doanh thu của NT1&2 trong năm 
2026 đạt 11,104 tỷ VND (5.3% YoY). Đối với dự án điện NT3&4, khoản thu nhập chênh 
lệch sản lượng theo giá công suất do sản lượng điện thực tế thấp hơn so với tỷ lệ Qc 
65% của nhà máy được duy trì sang 2H26 và 2027 sẽ giúp cho dự án ghi nhận doanh 
thu lần lượt 17,848/17,621 tỷ VND doanh thu trong 2026/2027.

Dựa trên định giá SOTP và EV/EBITDA, tri ển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các 
rủi ro có th ể phát sinh, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu POW. Giá m ục 
tiêu là 18,100 VND/cổ phiếu.

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco (AST) 
Tiếp tục mở rộng điểm kinh doanh 
22/09/2026  
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

KQKD tăng trư ởng tích c ực trong 
2Q2026

Tăng trư ởng khách qua c ảng hàng 
không thúc đ ẩy doanh thu t ại các 
điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu

Mở rộng mạng lưới tạo động lực tăng 
trưởng doanh thu mới

Cơ c ấu kinh doanh và hi ệu qu ả vận 
hành hỗ trợ cải thiện biên lợi nhuận

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA, giá mục 
tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, AST ghi nh ận doanh thu và LNST đ ạt 479/92 t ỷ VND (+22%/19% yoy), với 
động lực chủ yếu đến từ (1) đóng góp từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn 
Nhất và (2) tăng trưởng tích cực của các mảng hàng miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood 
và phòng ch ờ thương gia. Lũy k ế 6T2026, doanh thu và LNST đ ạt 1,035/221 t ỷ VND 
(+33%/46% yoy), lần lượt hoàn thành 48%/49% kế hoạch năm.

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa tại các thị trường AST khai thác tăng 
bình quân 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030. Lưu lượng và tỷ trọng khách quốc tế gia tăng 
được kỳ vọng thúc đẩy số giao dịch và doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi khách, đặc biệt 
tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

Chúng tôi dự phóng số điểm kinh doanh AST tăng từ 145 điểm cuối năm 2026 lên 182 
điểm năm 2030. Sau đóng góp t ừ 19 điểm tại T3 Tân Sơn Nh ất và 6 điểm mới tại T2 
Nội Bài từ 2025 - 2026, các dự án Long Thành, Phú Qu ốc và Gia Bình ti ếp tục mở ra 
dư địa mở rộng mạng lưới trong trung và dài hạn cho doanh nghiệp.

Việc mở rộng các hoạt động mang lại lợi nhuận cao, bao gồm hàng miễn thuế, phòng 
chờ thương gia và tối ưu hoạt động tại các điểm kinh doanh đ ược kỳ vọng tác động 
tích c ực lên biên l ợi nhu ận của AST . Chúng tôi dự phóng biên l ợi nhu ận g ộp đ ạt 
64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027, hỗ trợ LNST công ty mẹ tăng lần lượt 48%/17% 
yoy.

Chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với AST , giá mục tiêu 76 ,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương 
đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 21/09/2026.

Doanh thu 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ do 
không còn ghi nhận doanh thu 1 lần 
từ phí nước thải; trong khi LNST 
công ty mẹ tăng trưởng 1 chữ số

thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng nóng hơn; 
trong khi tri ển v ọng nhóm khách 
hàng công nghi ệp b ị ảnh hư ởng b ởi

Doanh thu 2Q2026 đạt 1,222 tỷ (-8% YoY/18.1% QoQ) và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 325 tỷ 
VND (7% YoY/55% QoQ), ghi nhận tăng trưởng của tất cả các mảng. Mảng cung cấp 
nước tăng trưởng ổn định, tiếp tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 785 
tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An v ận hành từ Q3/2025 giúp 
sản lượng nước tăng 28% YoY.

Cường độ và thời gian kéo dài c ủa các đ ợt nắng nóng trong 2026/2027 là cơ s ở để 
chúng tôi đi ều chỉnh tăng tăng trư ởng tiêu th ụ nước của nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phư ớc trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY. Trái lại, 
triển vọng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm sản xuất ảm đạm do tình hình thuế quan 
có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho các ngành xu ất khẩu truyền thống của Việt 
Nam.

UBND TP .HCM công bố đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyển và xử lý rác thải mới, tăng mạnh 
từ 50-150% nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh trong thực 
tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng điều này sẽ tác động tích cực với BWE khi các dự án của doanh 
nghiệp ở mảng này s ẽ vận hành hi ệu quả hơn. Dự báo doanh thu và l ợi nhuận gộp 
của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ VND lần lượt trong 
2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

cửa ngày 22/09/2026.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX điều chỉnh trở lại trong tuần qua sau khi gặp thử thách tại vùng cản quanh 1850, 
hình thành cây nến tuần giảm điểm nhưng với phần bóng dưới khá dài cho thấy sự tham 
gia tích cực của lực cầu bắt đáy tại vùng hỗ trợ gần. Trên khung ngày, nhịp giảm mạnh giữa 
tuần đã khiến chỉ số đánh mất đáy ngắn hạn tuần trước và chớm cắt xuống dưới SMA200, 
trong khi phiên hồi phục cuối tuần chưa cho thấy sự cải thiện rõ rệt của lực cầu. Mặc dù 
diễn biến này cho thấy áp lực điều chỉnh vẫn có thể còn tiếp diễn, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng 
tăng ngắn hạn chưa bị phá vỡ, VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ nhận được lực đỡ tại vùng hỗ trợ 
gần quanh 1750, tương ứng vùng hợp lưu quanh SMA50, và có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại. 
Kháng cự xa: 1880 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1830 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1750 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1710 (+-15)

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
IMF ANNUAL MEETINGS
Resilience, but no relief. The IMF's latest global growth forecast is un-
changed from a year ago and from April 2026 (at 3.1% in 2026 and 3.4%
in 2027), despite the energy shock, however with a different geogra -
phical breakdown: many energy importers and war-exposed countries
downgraded, and AI-exposed countries upgraded. Resilience was the

buzzword of the meetings, however it appeared mostly in the rear -
view mirror. Looking ahead, buffers are running thin, fiscal policy needs
urgent consolidation, and downside risks (from energy and AI exube -
rance) loom large. Global headline inflation is expected to rise to 4.7%
in 2026, mainly due to energy and food, before easing back to 4.1% in
2027 (revised +0.2pp). Growth in advanced economies is revised up to
2.1% in 2026 (+0.4pp, driven by Germany), staying at 1.8% in 2027. Eu-

rozone growth is upgraded to 1.1% in 2026 (+0.2pp) and 1.2% in 2027.
US growth stays projected at 2.3% in 2026 and 2027 (+0.1pp). Germany
sees the largest G7 upgrade, to 1.3% in 2026 (+0.6pp) and 1.1% in 2027
(+0.1pp). France is the only major advanced economy downgraded, to
0.4% in 2026 (-0.2pp) and 0.7% in 2027 (-0.2pp). For more detailed
takeaways see Substack and this week’s EcoWeek editorial.
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION
Trade policy, competitiveness and budget agreements by year-end.

EU leaders asked the Commission to propose by year-end an instru -
ment allowing, in extreme cases, full closure of the single market to
a rival deemed to be attacking the EU economy. It is designed as a
“second-strike” deterrent against unfair practices and retaliation.
The Commission is also exploring extending the hybrid-vehicle safe -
guards approach agreed with China last week to other sectors such
as chemicals and machinery. On the "Made in Europe" label, vehicles

would need to be assembled in Europe with at least 70% of compo -
nent value (excluding batteries) sourced locally and with substantial
R&D in the EU. The UK would be included, provided London aligns with
EU trade defence measures. EU Council also called for decisive pro -
gress on the competitiveness agenda, with the single-market (inclu -
ding 26th regime) and simplification files to be wrapped up by year-end.
On the multi-annual financial framework, the objective remains a deal

by end-2026, so that legislation can be adopted in 2027.
EURO AREA
Moderate tightening does it. Multiple ECB Governing Council members,
speaking from the IMF Meetings in Bangkok, re-underwrote their
“moderate response” approach to the ongoing energy-driven infla -
tion surge. Accordingly, they signaled that additional rate action was
likely but not imminent, acknowledging that the rise in bond yields
was doing part of the job, and that there were no signs yet of second

round effects. Strengthening the case for moderation, the Eurozone in-
dustrial production fell in August for the second month running, by
0.5% m/m, pulled down by pharmaceutical output and motor vehicles.
That said, production in Netherlands surged to a new high (2.5% m/m
and 8.7% y/y), driven by AI machinery and equipment.
France
Business starts booming, insolvencies are stable, growth outlook is
downgraded. Business creations rose by 16% y/y in the 3 rd quarter

(+23% y/y in September), driven mainly by the digital sector and retail.
In parallel, business bankruptcies stabilised in the 3rd quarter according
to Altares, with a 0.5% increase y/y (compared with a 5.4% increase y/y
in Q2), and their impact on employment declined: 50,510 jobs at risk
in Q3, down 3% y/y (see our recent analysis). The Banque de France
halved its estimate for Q3 GDP to 0.1% q/q (same as Insee) due to
the manufacturing sector. Its monthly survey showed a sharp jump in

business uncertainty in September, driven by fuel prices and economic
deterioration (the most acute readings in industry and services since
April). The OAT-bund spread was rangebound (137-142).
Germany
Producer prices are rising. Wholesale prices rose 8.7% y/y (vs. 6.8% in
August) due to higher wholesale prices for energy products and inter -
mediate goods, particularly metals.
UNITED KINGDOM
Summer outperformance and kudos from IMF. Monthly GDP defied

expectations in August and posted a 0.1% m/m against a -0.1% consen-
sus. This marked a slowdown from July (0.4% m/m), but also a fourth
consecutive month of expansion. Industrial production underpinned
the advance: it accelerated to 0.8% m/m (consensus: -0.1%, prior: 0.3%)
thanks to pharmaceuticals. The IMF sees growth accelerating to 1.3%
in 2026 (+0.3 pp since July) and 1.2% in 2027. It also singled out the UK
among advanced economies for its efforts and progress in putting its

fiscal house in order.
UNITED STATES
Inflation heats up, manufacturing accelerates, but an October hike
is unlikely. CPI inflation exceeded expectations at 0.65% m/m in Sep -
tember (up 0.2pp), the fastest monthly pace since April. Accordingly,
the y/y rate climbed to 3.7% (up 0.3pp). The biggest upside surprise,
however, came from the core CPI: at 0.33% m/m (consensus: 0.2%, prior:
0.3%) and 2.6% y/y (consensus: 2.5%, prior: 2.4%); as services remained

sticky while goods accelerated to 0.3% m/m on autos and AI-related
items. Producer prices too rose well above expectations to 1.3% m/m
(consensus: 0.6%, prior: 0.5%) – the largest monthly increase since
March 2022 – and 6.3% y/y (consensus: 5.3%, prior: 5.6%). Industrial
production accelerated in September to 0.7% m/m (up 0.6pp) thanks
to utilities. Manufacturing returned to growth (0.3% m/m, up 0.5pp)
with gains broadening beyond the AI-related and aerospace sectors.

Retail sales excl. autos and gasoline, by contrast, slowed sharply to
0.2% m/m (down 0.9pp). During his speech at the IMF Meetings after
the hotter than expected inflation prints, Chair Warsh declined to hint
at an imminent rate hike. We continue to expect the next rate hike to
occur in December.
To watch this week:
- Developed markets Flash PMIs for October (Friday).
- Eurozone Germany September PPI, October ZEW (Tuesday), France Insee Business Climate (Thursday).

- UK August Employment data (Tuesday), September CPI and PPI (Wednesday), September Retail Sales (Friday).
- Japan September CPI, October PMI (Friday).

### Page 2

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
JAPAN
Producer price inflation eases. Producer prices fell for a second month
in a row in September, down a further 0.5% m/m, while the yearly rate
slowed for the first time since February (-0.8pp), though it remains
firm at 6.8%. The IMF forecasts growth at 0.7% in both 2026 and 2027,
broadly unchanged. The yen weakened past 158 per dollar again after

reports that the BoJ is leaning against a back-to-back October rate hike.
EMERGING ECONOMIES
Emerging markets
Asia’s outlook upgraded, other regions downgraded.
The IMF upgraded its Asia growth forecast to 4.8% in 2026 and 4.4% in
2027. The largest upgrades are for economies well integrated into the
AI supply chain, notably Taiwan (+5.5pp to 10.7%), South Korea (+0.7pp
to 3.3%), Singapore, Vietnam, Malaysia and to some extent Thailand.
India is forecast to grow 7% in both 2026 and 2027 (fiscal year basis),

up 0.6 and 0.3pp from July on robust domestic demand.
Central Europe’s GDP growth is projected at 2.1% in 2026, slightly
lower than April’s forecast. In 2027, it remains unchanged at 2.3%.
Growth paths are diverse within the region.
In the Middle East & North Africa, GDP is expected to contract by 2%
in 2026. GCC real GDP should fall by 4.8% with only Oman expected
to post positive growth (4.1%). A marked rebound is expected for 2027
(MENA: 7%; GCC: 9.1%).

The growth outlook for Latin America remains subdued, revised down
to 2.3% in 2026 (from 2.4%) and 2.5% in 2027 (from 2.7%). However, in
its "International Trade Outlook 2026" published on 15 October, the
Economic Commission for Latin America and the Caribbean (ECLAC) ex-
pects the region to experience a trade boom in 2026: goods exports are
projected to surge by 20% in value terms (11% from price increases and
9% from volume growth), with trade to Asia (excluding China) soaring

by 32%, while intraregional trade growth lags behind at 8%. Yet, these
export gains are concentrated in commodities and low-value-added
manufacturing, limiting their spillover to broader growth. Stronger
trade is thus providing only a modest boost to the economy.
China
Explosive export growth, subdued inflation and credit growth. In Sep-
tember, export value growth surged to 36.3% y/y from 25% in August,
driven by semiconductors, computer equipment and other AI goods, as

well as green technologies. Export prices surged, especially for semi -
conductors, while total export volumes remained robust. Import value
growth also accelerated to 31% y/y from 28.2% in August, boosted by
both rising volume and rapidly increasing prices. The trade surplus
hit a new monthly record of USD 134 bn in September. CPI inflation
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, mainly because of a 14.9% rise in
vehicle fuel prices. Food prices continued to fall (-1.7% y/y) and core

inflation was stable at 1%. Producer price inflation accelerated to 5%
(the highest since 2022) due to rising commodity prices. Growth in to-
tal domestic credit outstanding (“social financing”) continued to slow
down, reaching a record low of 7.1% y/y in September. Bank loans to
households declined (-2% y/y) for the seventh consecutive month.
In Q3 2026, real GDP growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y (1.2% q/q) from
4.3% y/y (0.9% q/q), primarily led by industrial production (which rose by

5.1% y/y on average in Q3 vs. 4.6% in Q2). On the demand side, exports
were the key driver while fixed-asset investment continued to contract.
Retail sales posted a small pickup in September but remained flat
in real terms in Q3 as a whole. With growth reaching 4.6% y/y year-to-
date, China needs only a 4.2% growth in Q4 to meet the official target of
4.5%-5% for this year – which seems easily feasible. The IMF trimmed
its growth forecast by 0.1pp to 4.5% in 2026 and 4% in 2027.

Indonesia
MSCI launched a consultation on reclassifying the country from Emer-
ging Market to Standalone Market status, with a final decision due by
end-June 2027, citing opaque shareholding structures and suspected
coordinating trading. Since January 2026, MSCI had frozen Index ad -
justments and threatened only a downgrade to Frontier market status
(like Pakistan), but market participants argued that a Frontier reclas -
sification would not properly reflect the extent of the market’s pro -

blems. Over the medium term, the risk is heavy foreign capital outflows
at a time when net FDI no longer covers the current account deficit.
This leaves Indonesia reliant on foreign portfolio inflows and the ru -
piah exposed to a reversal in sentiment. On the day of this announce -
ment, the Jakarta Composite Index fell by 11.6%, its steepest drop since
January 1998. Among Asian emerging markets, Indonesia has posted
the worst stock market performance since the beginning of the year

(-40.7%).
COMMODITIES
Iran/Oil. More of the same. Brent oil prices have remained broadly
stable this week (0.5% at 107$/b on Monday). Renewed Iranian at -
tacks on tankers in the Strait and intensification of Houthis’ attack over
the Saudi territory feed the geopolitical risk premium on barrel prices.
But IEA announcement of an accelerated 100 mb oil release (above
the March initial target of 400 mb) and reports that the US may lift
sanctions on the Russian and Iranian shadow fleets have contributed to

crude price stability. The spread with physical barrel (Dated Brent) has
declined (6.7$/b on Oct 16th). Trump administration’s various attempts
to lower diesel prices have had a limited impact for now. European gas
prices (TTF) recorded another weekly rise (4.7% w/w to 88.4 €/MWh,
the highest level since Dec 22nd, 2022) on the back of the rising number
of attacks on vessels in the Gulf and persisting disruption in the Strait.
However, EU gas inventories reached 74% of full capacities on October

16th (i.e., almost at the 80% month-end target), which should reduce
support for TTF prices going forward.

Document 296: Eurozone _ Growth on track despite the turbulence (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone _ Growth on track despite the turbulence (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 2

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank

GROWTH ON TRACK DESPITE THE TURBULENCE
Growth was stronger than expected in Q2 and is set to strengthen further in the coming quarters. According to our fore-
casts, it will reach 1.5% in 2027 after 1.1% in 2026. This growth is expected to be driven by investment and its knock-on
effect on exports. On an annual average basis, inflation is expected to stand at 3% in 2026 and 2.8% in 2027, peaking at

the end of 2026. The ECB is expected to continue its monetary tightening. At this stage, a single further rate rise, aimed at
keeping inflation expectations anchored, is expected in December. The impact of rising interest rates (key policy rates and
market rates) on public finances and private sector borrowing conditions will be worth monitoring.
GROWTH: BEYOND THE SHOCKS, A RESILIENT MOMENTUM
Growth in the Eurozone remained robust in the first half of 2026, at

0.25% q/q in Q1 and 0.33% q/q in Q2, excluding Ireland (where economic
activity is volatile). It was driven by Germany, where signs of a recovery
are becoming clearer (see Germany). Our baseline scenario forecasts
growth of 1.1% in 2026, followed by 1.5% in 2027 on an annual average
basis. This growth is expected to be driven by the strengthening of
trends already underway in public and private investment (particularly
in defence and AI) and in exports (trade in services and intra-zone trade

in goods). Household consumption is likely to be resilient too. According
to our forecasts, job creation would continue to underpin household
incomes, thereby cushioning the impact of inflation.
A NEW LEASE OF LIFE FOR INDUSTRY
The Eurozone is undergoing one of the most profound industrial trans-
formations in its history. This is giving rise to divergences between key
sectors but, above all, to new drivers of growth whose effects are beco-
ming increasingly apparent (see our analysis). Indeed, the signs of an

economic recovery are growing stronger. Business sentiment indicators
show a marked improvement in the economic outlook across most sec-
tors (Chart 2), driven primarily by electronics (AI, digitalisation), the
automotive sector and aerospace. European industry is thus benefi -
ting from strong demand for capital goods; a structural trend that will
continue to be fuelled by ongoing investment cycles in AI, defence and
electrification. The introduction of minimum European content quotas

in the draft law on the industrial accelerator, together with the Com -
mission’s intention to tighten the criteria for access to European public
procurement markets, will provide additional support for European in-
dustry in the longer term.
FOREIGN TRADE: A REPOSITIONING THAT IS PAYING OFF
Following a decline in 2026, linked to rising energy import costs, the
trade surplus is expected to rise to around 3.0% of GDP in 2027 (of
which 2% of GDP for goods and 1% for services). Growth in European ex-

ports has slowed compared with its pre-Covid trend and is being ham-
pered by the decline in Chinese and US markets. However, Eurozone
exports in volume rose significantly in Q2 (+3.4% q/q).
More importantly, since Liberation Day (April 2025), Eurozone coun -
tries have found significant sources of growth amongst other European
partners, which has enabled them to cushion the decline in exports to
certain third markets. This is evident in exports to the rest of the EU,

EFTA countries (Switzerland in particular) and, to a lesser extent, the
United Kingdom, Ukraine and the Balkans (Chart 3). The momentum of
intra-European trade therefore remains very strong. It is expected to
strengthen further with the anticipated investments in defence, AI and
electrification.
FINANCING: FINANCIAL CONDITIONS ARE EXPECTED TO REMAIN TIGHT
Financial conditions in the Eurozone remained tight in Q2 2026, accor-
ding to indices calculated by the ECB (including the BIG [Broad Inter -

mediation Gauge]). They are expected to tighten more significantly in
the second half of the year: i/ the rise in long-term bond yields, which
has accelerated since the outbreak of the conflict in Iran, is expected to
continue; and ii/ the ECB will continue its monetary tightening (+25 bps

world
in Q3, then in Q4). For the time being, the debt burden is still weighing
only moderately on public finances due to the long average maturity of
countries’ debt (8.2 years on average in the Eurozone in July), but this
burden will increase by 2030.
On the corporate front, the rise in interest rates has remained contained
(+2 bps since January 2026, reaching 3.9% in July), which has contri -

buted to the strong growth in new investment loans (cumulated over
one year, +9.1% y/y in July compared with 15.3% y/y in September 2025).
By contrast, cumulative net debt securities issues over one year re -
mained stable in July, whilst cumulative net issues of listed shares over
one year halved month on month.
Rising interest rates will continue to hamper new variable-rate
home loans to households (around 65% of new lending) more signi -
ficantly than fixed-rate loans, for which rates stood in July at 3.69%

(+4 bps m/m) and 3.36% (+2 bps m/m) respectively. New home loans,
which are stable in 2026 on a cumulative over one year basis, are there-
fore unlikely to see any improvement. A fall in real-estate transactions
could weigh on new consumer credit through a reduction in expenditure
on home furnishings and renovations, whereas consumer credit has
been fairly buoyant so far (year-to-date, +5.2% y/y in July compared
with +1.4% y/y in January).
LABOUR MARKET: RESILIENCE YET TO BE CONFIRMED

Employment in the Eurozone rose again by an average of 0.1% q/q in
the first half of 2026, driven by Spain and Portugal. Given the growth
outlook in these two countries, this momentum is likely to continue,
whilst business sentiment surveys in Germany suggest that employ -
ment is beginning to stabilise. Nevertheless, employment levels are
plateauing (France, the Netherlands) or even falling in other countries
(Austria, Finland), which is likely to slow the decline in the Eurozone

unemployment rate, meaning it is expected to remain close to current
levels in 2027. Furthermore, second-round effects of the rise in inflation
on wages cannot be ruled out: the ECB’s wage tracker suggests wage
growth of close to 3% by summer 2027 (2.9% y/y in June 2027, compared
with 2.6% in September).
INFLATION: A PEAK EXPECTED IN LATE 2026
Changes in wage dynamics will be a key factor in the inflation out -
look for 2027, whilst energy price rises are picking up again, driven

by the conflict in the Middle East. This rise is even sharper than in
the spring for gas (linked to restocking ahead of winter) and is likely
to feed through to electricity prices in several countries. We therefore
anticipate inflation peaking at 3.7% y/y in Q4 2026, before easing as the
energy component falls. On an annual average, headline inflation is
expected to stand at 3% in 2026 and then at 2.8% in 2027.
At this stage, the pass-through of rising energy prices to other com -

ponents of the consumer price index, particularly services, remains li -
mited. The alternative core indicators monitored by the ECB, including
the PCCI (Persistent and Common Component of Inflation, 2.1% in July),
remain close to the 2% target. Core inflation, however, is expected to
peak two months later than headline inflation (3% in March 2027).
Inflationary risks are nevertheless tilted to the upside. These are lin -
ked to food prices, which are expected to be affected by the energy

shock and weather conditions (summer drought, El Niño); they could
also stem from a delayed impact of energy price rises on underlying
inflation. The rise in prices of energy-dependent inputs (notably che -
micals and plastics) and those linked to the AI sector is expected to be
sustained. Growth, which we expect to remain robust over the coming
quarters, will also fuel underlying inflationary pressures.
MONETARY POLICY: TIGHTENING WITHOUT SLOWING (GROWTH)

Following two 25-basis-point rises (in June and September), we ex -
pect a third in December, which would bring the deposit facility rate
to 2.75%. It would then be in slightly restrictive territory (we estimate
the upper bound of the neutral rate to be 2.5%). This rise would be
aimed primarily at anchoring inflation expectations and, consequently,
at curbing the pass-through effects of inflation. Monetary tightening
would therefore remain limited. Indeed, the overshoot of the inflation

target would be temporary, and the operational framework adopted by
the ECB in June 2025 allows it to tolerate this, provided it remains brief.
However, it cannot be ruled out that the ECB might take further action
if the decline in inflation proves slower than expected.
PUBLIC FINANCES ARE CONTINGENT ON RISING INTEREST RATES
The fiscal impulse in the Eurozone is expected to be clearly positive in
2026 due to the rise in defence and infrastructure spending in Germany.

This will contribute to a widening of the country’s primary deficit (from
+0.9 pp of GDP to 2.8%, according to our forecasts). The improvement in
public finances is expected to stall in both France and Italy, at least in
2026, due to the impact of inflation on the debt burden via index-linked
bonds. This trend is expected to reverse in 2027 with less expansionary
fiscal policies. Nevertheless, the increase in the debt service burden
would fuel a further rise in the public deficit, which would thus reach

around 3.3% and 3.5% of GDP in 2026 and 2027 respectively (compared
with 2.9% in 2025). Against this backdrop, the Eurozone’s public debt-
to-GDP ratio is projected to exceed 90% of GDP in 2026 and reach 93%
of GDP in 2027, according to our forecasts.
Article completed on 15 September 2026

Document 303: Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21)

Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3

### Page 1

21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP ,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.

Document 315: How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-23
